西站发车令第四幕 / 西站调车场 / 战役对白 / 安娜（0419）／常态

出场或被记录人物　阿列克谢、随行伙伴、安娜

查看地图

  1. 旁白 西站调度室外的防寒铁皮板上，孤零零地钉着一张还没来得及撕毁的绝密发车令，上面内务部的红章新鲜得发黏，像一滩刚刚凝固的血：

    旁白 【特别运输指令 · 严禁悬挂车次牌 · 发车时刻：23:00 · 行车区间：鹰嘴崖死线 · 第三节车厢核载：176 人 · 死令：实毁数与账面数必须绝对闭合】

    旁白 你拍下那个鲜红的印鉴。报废守车的工具箱底躺着一支拆开的旧莫辛纳甘。枪托裂了，枪机抹着黄油。你把它装好；枪膛里还剩三发。

    安娜 把「第三节」再念一遍。我怕一慌就记岔。

    English

    A dispatch order is still pinned to the board outside the control room, the red stamp still tacky.

    [SPECIAL RUN · NO TRAIN NUMBER · 23:00 · EAGLE'S CRAG LINE · BOXCAR 3 · 176 PERSONS · PHYSICAL COUNT MUST EQUAL LEDGER]

    You photograph the fresh red stamp. At the bottom of the dead brake van's toolbox lies a disassembled Mosin–Nagant. The stock is split, and the bolt is greased. You fit it together; three rounds remain.

    Say 'Boxcar Three' again. I don't want to get it wrong if I panic.

    选项与回执
    1. 带走莫辛纳甘，抄下车次时刻

      红章下面是 23:00。你把时刻抄在袖口，把莫辛纳甘背上肩。鹰嘴崖的路已经找到了。

      English

      Take the Mosin–Nagant and copy the timetable Beneath the red stamp: 23:00. You copy the time onto your cuff and sling the Mosin–Nagant over your shoulder. The road to Eagle's Crag is marked.

    旁白 你拍下那个鲜红的印鉴。报废守车的工具箱底躺着一支拆开的旧莫辛纳甘。枪托裂了，枪机抹着黄油。你把它装好；枪膛里还剩三发。

    老轨伊万 路我照样带。过道岔时你走前头，我得看得见你的手。

    English

    A dispatch order is still pinned to the board outside the control room, the red stamp still tacky.

    [SPECIAL RUN · NO TRAIN NUMBER · 23:00 · EAGLE'S CRAG LINE · BOXCAR 3 · 176 PERSONS · PHYSICAL COUNT MUST EQUAL LEDGER]

    You photograph the fresh red stamp. At the bottom of the dead brake van's toolbox lies a disassembled Mosin–Nagant. The stock is split, and the bolt is greased. You fit it together; three rounds remain.

    I'll still show you the way. You go first at the points. I need to see your hands.

    选项与回执
    1. 带走莫辛纳甘，抄下车次时刻

      红章下面是 23:00。你把时刻抄在袖口，把莫辛纳甘背上肩。鹰嘴崖的路已经找到了。

      English

      Take the Mosin–Nagant and copy the timetable Beneath the red stamp: 23:00. You copy the time onto your cuff and sling the Mosin–Nagant over your shoulder. The road to Eagle's Crag is marked.

南侧编组线第五幕 / 布拉风货场 · 南侧编组线 / 战役对白

出场或被记录人物　阿列克谢

查看地图

  1. 阿列克谢 表格只写了发车，没有写到站。交接底单还在车尾；没有销账章，就不能说车里的人已经死了。

    阿列克谢 那就把底单带到鹰嘴崖。今晚不再让一串數字替我回答。

    English

    The forms record the departure, not the arrival. The handover copy remains by the rear cars; without a closure stamp, nobody can say those aboard are dead.

    Then we take the copy to Eagle's Crag. Tonight I will not let a row of numbers answer for me.

    选项与回执
    1. 先去检修柜查交接底单

      底单还在检修柜里。你记下位置，准备去抄。

      English

      Copy the handover and mark the maintenance route Find the copy in the dispatch locker, then bring the route back.

鹰嘴崖进场第五幕 / 鹰嘴崖 · 山谷检修线 / 战役对白

出场或被记录人物　阿列克谢

查看地图

  1. 阿列克谢 鹰嘴崖的车轮停了。莫辛纳甘背好，先找到第三节车厢，把门打开。

    旁白 门后还有人在敲。崖顶的灯正往这边扫。

    English

    The wheels have stopped at Eagle's Crag. The Mosin is slung and ready. Find Boxcar Three and open it.

    Someone is still knocking from inside. The ridge light is sweeping this way.

    选项与回执
    1. 沿检修线靠近第三节车厢

      你沿检修线走向车尾。门后的敲击声停了一下，又响起来。

      English

      Follow the maintenance line to Boxcar Three You follow the maintenance line toward the rear cars. The knocking pauses, then starts again.

302 广播塔第六幕 / 302 高地 · 广播塔 / 战役对白

出场或被记录人物　阿列克谢、拉娜

查看地图

  1. 阿列克谢 把底单和庄园照片接进播音台。证词不能只剩一串数字。

    阿列克谢 名字念出去，家属就知道该找谁。清杀队也会听见。

    拉娜 哥……如果塔门守不住呢？

    阿列克谢 发射机只要再响一分钟，够全城听见就够了。

    English

    Feed the handover copy and Manor photographs into the board. The testimony cannot be reduced to numbers.

    Once the names are aired, families will know where to look. OSK will hear them too.

    Alyosha… what if the door gives?

    Keep the transmitter alive for one more minute. If the city hears it, that is enough.

    选项与回执
    1. 完整播出证词和幸存者名单

      你合上发射机总闸。守住南门，直到名单传完。

      English

      Broadcast the testimony and every survivor's name You bring the transmitter online. Hold the south gate until the list goes through.

    2. 只播罪证，切断个人姓名

      你决定只播照片和档案编号。到播音台后，等证据发完再走。

      English

      Broadcast the evidence, omit the names You decide to air only the photographs and file numbers. Stay at the desk until transmission is complete.

      你合上发射机总闸。守住南门，直到名单传完。

      English

      Broadcast the testimony and every survivor's name You bring the transmitter online. Hold the south gate until the list goes through.

    2. 只播罪证，切断个人姓名

      你决定只播照片和档案编号。到播音台后，等证据发完再走。

      English

      Broadcast the evidence, omit the names You decide to air only the photographs and file numbers. Stay at the desk until transmission is complete.

暖气井回退者夜潜 / 旧版遭遇面板

抉择 A · 你是否还承认他是人

English

CHOICE A · Do you still call him human?

暖气井里蜷着一个半人。他的瞳孔还映着灯，身体却被高烧烧得发抖；对面楼顶的红外点正慢慢移过来。你若承认他是人，接下来每一个动作都要付出代价。

English

A half-human curls in the heating shaft, pupils still reflecting the light while fever shakes him. An IR dot crawls across the opposite roof. If you call him human, every next action has a price.

A1 冒险喂阿司匹林与温水，等他醒来（各消耗 1）

English

A1 Risk aspirin and warm water; wait for him to wake (1 each)

　你身上没有阿司匹林和热水。

English

　You have no aspirin or water.

A2 贴墙绕行，把他的哭声留给黑暗（良知 −2）

English

A2 Slip past and leave his crying to the dark (Conscience −2)

A3 一撬棍让他安静，再搜走罐头（良知 −10 · 卌 +1）

English

A3 Silence him, take his tin (Conscience −10 · stroke +1)

按 Esc 先不决定（他会继续烧）。

English

Esc to decide later (he keeps burning).

清杀队 · 追杀夜潜 / 清杀队

是夜潜者！焊死前头路口，绝不能让他钻回居住区！

English

Target spotted! Seal the junction. Do not let him reach the blocks!

他插翅难逃！二号从涵洞包抄，探照灯死死钉住他！

English

He will not escape. Two, flank him. Keep the light on him!

站住！再往前一步，按灰鳞狂暴者就地销账！

English

Halt! Run again and we fire!

清杀队 · 警戒夜潜 / 清杀队

English

Who is there? Step into the light. Papers, now.

不准动！手放在光柱里，别让我提醒第二遍。

English

Do not move. Keep your hands where I can see them.

宵禁封锁区。转过身，面朝铁墙慢慢退过来！

English

This sector is sealed. Turn around and approach slowly.

酒馆里的换药人运行时事件资源 / 触发地点随遭遇规则

黑市篝火旁，前市立中学语文教师马卡尔当着众人的面，把古丽娅给他女儿退烧的两粒阿司匹林换成半瓶掺水工业酒精，仰头灌下，又跪在雪泥里揪着头发狂笑：「来啊，往我脸上吐唾沫！我是个吃女儿肉的畜生！可她熬过四天又能怎样？醒过来还是要被塞进铁壳号！我喝掉这两粒药，是因为我连看她清醒着被烧死的勇气都没有！阿列克谢，你撬得开铁门，撬得开人心里这口棺材吗？！」地窖里，他的女儿仍在四十度高烧中喊冷；他越把这句话喊得响亮，越像是在等谁替他把最后一丝做人的资格也拿走。

English

At the market fire, former schoolteacher Makar trades the two aspirin meant for his feverish daughter for diluted industrial alcohol. He begs the crowd to spit on him: even if she survives, the Buran will take her. His self-abasement is both confession and refuge.

选项与回执
  1. 用自己的抗生素换回那半瓶酒精

    马卡尔捏着你给他的药，忽然不笑了。他把酒精推回来，像推开一具自己的尸体：「你为什么现在才来？我已经把最不要脸的话说完了……」他抱着药爬回地窖，脚步仍旧踉跄。你没有见证退烧的奇迹，只替那个孩子留住了一次尚不确定的天亮。

    English

    Trade your own antibiotics for the bottle You trade your antibiotics for the bottle. Makar crawls back to the cellar; no miracle is promised, only another uncertain dawn.

  2. 夺下酒瓶，拖他回女儿身边

    他一边同你争夺酒瓶，一边恳求你再打得重些，仿佛疼痛是唯一不必付费的赦罪。你最终把他拖回地窖门口；他不敢进去，只隔着门听见女儿烧得说胡话，便跪在雪里号啕大哭。门没有因此变成天堂，但他至少没有再逃走。

    English

    Take the bottle and drag him home You drag him to the cellar door. He cannot enter, but he no longer runs; outside, he weeps at the sound of his daughter's fever.

  3. 让他跪在雪里，不替他裁决

    你转身走了。身后的人还在大声咒骂自己，仿佛只要抢先把自己骂成畜生，别人的审判就再也伤不到他。你没有唾弃他，也没有原谅他；你只是忽然害怕，他那套听上去无比丑恶的账，自己在某个夜里也曾悄悄算过。

    English

    Leave him in the snow without judging him You walk away without spitting or absolving. His self-hatred sounds obscene because some part of you recognises the arithmetic.

冻僵的孩子运行时事件资源 / 触发地点随遭遇规则

结满冰溜子的楼梯间死角里，一个瘦小的女孩蜷缩在水泥墙根下，冻得乌青的嘴唇只剩下一丝若有若无的白气。

English

A child huddles in the stairwell, barely breathing from the cold.

选项与回执
  1. 把热水壶留给她

    她用冻得像胡萝卜似的小手死死抱住温热的水壶，贴在心口，青紫的嘴唇上终于泛起了一丝活人的血色。

    English

    Leave her the hot water Colour returns to her lips.

  2. 分她半把黑麦

    你把粗糙的冷黑麦碎粒塞进她僵硬的手心，她连雪带米一口吞了下去，仰起脸冲你流下一行无声的泪。

    English

    Share some grits You press the grits into her hand.

  3. 什么都不留，走开

    你狠下心裹紧大衣快步走上台阶，身后那点微弱得像小猫一样的抽泣声，在风雪里一点点彻底冷了下去。

    English

    Leave her nothing The crying behind you fades.

黑暗里的半人运行时事件资源 / 触发地点随遭遇规则

伸手不见五指的废墟死角里，传来退化半人抱头撞墙的痛苦呜咽，他正陷在高热的神经风暴里，还没有察觉你的气息。

English

A regressed half-human whimpers in the dark; it has not seen you.

选项与回执
  1. 扔空罐把它引开

    空铁皮罐头在远处的冰面上砸出清脆的回响，那个可怜的影子笨拙地转过身，循着响动跌跌撞撞地摸了过去。

    English

    Throw a can to lure it away It follows the sound away.

  2. 屏息贴墙摸过去

    你死死咬住冻僵的嘴唇，贴着滴水的冰冷砖墙，像一抹真正的幽灵般从他颤抖的脊背旁一寸寸挪了过去。

    English

    Hold your breath and slip past You inch along the wall.

  3. 挥刀先发制人

    冰冷的铁器带着风声狠狠劈落，那阵令人心烦意乱的呜咽声戛然而止——黑暗重新归于死寂，只剩你自己的喘息。

    English

    Strike first The blade falls; the crying stops.

锅炉房镜子里的核算员运行时事件资源 / 触发地点随遭遇规则

午夜一点，你刚在《双面工作日志》背面划下一道血痕，锅炉房结霜的破镜里便走出另一个阿列克谢：蓝袖章、雪亮眼镜、体面铁路制服，连指甲缝也没有煤灰。他坐上蒸汽管，温和得像来探病的旧友：「阿廖沙，何必呢？拉娜已经是一行 0420 的油墨，你救不出墨水。把古丽娅那三页废纸交给督察官吧，你能换两箱瑞士 409 批腹透液，还能升任二等核算主任。妈能多活三年，你也能睡在有暖气的屋子里——做个合乎规矩的庸人有什么错？为什么非要当个满手冻疮的圣徒？」他没有辱骂你，也没有撒谎；正因如此，你才想立刻杀死他。

English

At 01:00 a blue-armbanded Aleksei steps from the frosted boiler-room mirror: clean uniform, bright glasses, no soot beneath his nails. Trade Gulya's pages for dialysis fluid and promotion, he says; Lana is already ink. His gentleness is worse than mockery because none of his arithmetic is false.

选项与回执
  1. 回答：拉娜不是一行油墨

    你一拳砸向那张温和的笑脸。镜子裂开，每一片里却仍坐着一个戴蓝袖章的你；血流进掌纹时，他们齐声纠正：「是 0420。」你疼得发抖，却第一次敢直视那些碎片：「不。她先是我妹妹，然后才是你们的一行字。」白昼的幻象消失了，冻疮和罪却一样也没有少。

    English

    Answer: Lana is not a line of ink You strike the mirror. Every fragment answers "0420," but you insist she was your sister before she was their ink. The vision vanishes; your wounds and guilt remain.

  2. 承认：我只想做个合乎规矩的庸人

    镜中的人满意地点头，把一张二等核算主任的任命书贴在玻璃另一侧。可你伸手去接时，摸到的仍是自己冰凉的指尖。没有两箱腹透液，没有暖房，也没有谁真来赦免你；只有你终于承认，所谓庸人的幸福，有时不过是不再追问别人被运去了哪里。

    English

    Admit: I only want to be ordinary and obedient You accept his respectable appointment and find only your own cold fingertips. The comfort is real for one moment: the comfort of no longer asking where the trains go.

  3. 跪下，求那个体面的自己替你决定

    你跪下来，求他告诉你究竟怎样才算无罪。蓝袖章的幻影俯身扶你，动作温柔得像一位兄长：「你看，阿廖沙，连你的反抗也需要我批准。只要你还只想证明自己是个好人，你就永远是我的。」你猛然抬头，镜前却只剩一个人跪着，分不清刚才被扶起的是身体，还是最后一点抵抗。

    English

    Kneel and let your respectable self decide You beg him for innocence. He gently replies that even your rebellion needs his approval so long as you only wish to prove yourself good. When you look up, only one man is kneeling.

调度室的两难运行时事件资源 / 触发地点随遭遇规则

废弃调度室结霜的木桌上摊着两份还没来得及归档的电传底单，外头巡逻队的靴声已近，时间只够你抓走其中一份。

English

Two slips lie on the desk; you have time to take only one.

选项与回执
  1. 拿走焚化炉零件清单

    你一把扯下零件清单塞进怀里——上面用红铅笔圈着几个尚未被拆走的军工级精钢部件。

    English

    Take the furnace parts list It lists parts still unclaimed.

  2. 拿走药品调拨单

    你抓走了盖着红印的药品调拨单——凭着这页薄纸，还能从黑市医务站里换出一批救命的盘尼西林。

    English

    Take the medical chit The chit trades for medicine.

蒸汽管破裂运行时事件资源 / 触发地点随遭遇规则

身旁一截生锈的市政高压蒸汽管突然崩裂，滚烫刺耳的白雾夹着铁锈味喷涌而出，一旦惊动远处的哨兵便万劫不复。

English

A steam pipe bursts beside you, scalding white fog pouring out.

选项与回执
  1. 用废钢件临时封堵

    你抄起沉重的废钢件狠命砸扁了翻卷的管口，刺耳的嘶鸣声渐渐平息，白雾终于止住了。

    English

    Patch with scrap steel You hammer the crack shut.

  2. 撕下衣袖硬堵

    你扯下厚棉衣袖死命按在裂口上，滚烫的蒸汽一次次把破布顶开，又被你烫起水泡的双手硬生生按了回去。

    English

    Tear your sleeve and plug The rag holds, barely.

  3. 放任不管，继续走

    你没有停留，任由那股救命的供暖白雾在身后的极夜里白白喷涌、消散。

    English

    Leave it and move on The white cloud keeps billowing.

被楔死的门运行时事件资源 / 触发地点随遭遇规则

一扇厚重的铁皮木门被人从里面用木楔死死顶住，透过结霜的门缝，隐约能看见屋角散落着几罐还没开封的配给物资。

English

A door is wedged from inside; loot is visible through the gap.

选项与回执
  1. 用撬棍暴力破门

    道钉撬棍带着回音「哐当」一声砸烂了门闩，你在满地碎木屑里翻出了前人留下的救命存货。

    English

    Force it with the crowbar The door slams open.

  2. 慢慢拆下楔子

    你屏住呼吸，用撬棍扁平的尖端顺着门缝一点点拨动，没发出一丝惊动暗处的声响便退出了木楔。

    English

    Work the wedge out quietly You ease the wedge out.

  3. 放弃这扇门

    你深深看了一眼门缝里的暗影，没有去惊扰那扇门后的死寂，转身继续向黑暗深处走去。

    English

    Leave the door You turn and move on.

绿灯、车皮与签名0-1 / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 调度室里的暖气片半凉不热，铁管里不时传出水垢被冲刷的敲击声。窗外的广轨编组站正被白毛风裹得严严实实，一列刷着防化绿漆的重型装甲车头正在顶推车皮，车顶两挺重机枪在雪里凝成了两个白疙瘩——那是“布拉风号”，站上的老工人都管它叫“铁壳号”。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 墙上用图钉扎着本周配额进度表，蓝墨水画的折线在末端耷拉下去。红铅笔批注压在末尾：【第17区人员转运编组完成率61%：连续两周不达标，市政供暖降温40%】。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 调度长用指甲剔了剔牙缝，把一张三联复写单推到阿列克谢面前。单子旁边压着区管委后勤科的一页便签，上面盖着褪色的椭圆章：瑞士 409 批腹膜透析液，超额留用，配额一箱（6袋）。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢的母亲卡佳在 602 室已经两天没能排出毒素，双腿肿得像泡发的面团，皮肤薄得发亮，一碰就渗黄水。

调度长 （把印泥盒推过来，铁盒盖在桌面上蹭出一道白印） 内务部要把 004 号闷罐编进铁壳号，走固定循环。送完一批，空车拉回来接下一批。底单在电传机里。你签个字，本周指标算咱们超额。你妈那一箱药，明早头一班吉斯卡车送进你们楼。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢扶了扶眼镜。左边镜腿用黑绝缘胶布缠了三圈，胶布脱胶，黏在耳根上发痒。他凑近看了看底单。

阿列克谢 （钢笔尖悬在纸上，离纸面不到半寸） 品名……疫区留观人员及陪护，一百七十六名。 ……调度长，一百七十六个活人，怎么能按‘零号散货’走账？

调度长 （眼皮都没抬一下） 莫罗佐夫，电传机认代码，不认人。你签了，他们是一百七十六个转运编号；你不签，全楼供暖明晚掐掉两截，你妈连明天中午都熬不过去。你自己掂量。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 门口的皮帘子被掀开，灌进一股刺骨的煤焦油味。“老轨”伊万提着防风信号灯跨进来，身上落满了硬雪碴子。老头吐掉嘴里烧尽的烟蒂，靴底在水泥地上跺得震天响。

“老轨”伊万 （从阿列克谢背后走过，看都没看桌上的单子） 这节车皮还会回来。你要是签了，名字就跟着随车联一起铆在门上。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 伊万掀帘子出去了，穿堂风把桌上的复写纸掀起一个角。

English

The radiator in the dispatch office was half-warm at best; now and then a knock travelled through the iron pipes as scale was flushed loose. Outside, the broad-gauge marshalling yard was sealed tight in the white squall. A heavy armoured locomotive painted in anti-chemical green was shunting boxcars, two heavy machine guns on its roof frozen into white lumps in the snow — the armoured train Buran, which the old hands at the station called the Iron Shell.

This week's quota chart was pinned to the wall with drawing pins, its blue-ink line sagging at the end. A red-pencil annotation lay pressed beneath it: [District 17 personnel-transfer marshalling, 61% complete: second consecutive week below target; municipal heating reduced by 40%].

The dispatcher picked at his teeth with a fingernail and slid a triplicate carbon form across to Aleksei. Beside it lay a memo from the logistics section of the District Rationing Committee, stamped with a faded oval seal: Swiss Batch 409 peritoneal dialysis fluid, retained beyond quota, one case allocated (6 bags).

For two days now his mother Katya, in Room 602, had been unable to purge the toxins from her body. Her legs were swollen like risen dough, the skin thin and shiny, weeping yellow fluid at a touch.

Aleksei adjusted his glasses. The left arm had been wrapped three times with black electrical tape; the adhesive had gone tacky and it itched against the base of his ear. He leaned closer to read the Dispatch Slip.

Description... persons under observation from the infected zone and their attendants, one hundred and seventy-six. ...Dispatcher, one hundred and seventy-six living people — how can they be booked as 'zero-grade bulk freight'?

Morozov, the teletype reads codes, not people. You sign, and they become one hundred and seventy-six transfer numbers. You don't, and two heating lines get cut off for the whole building tomorrow night — your mother won't last till noon. Weigh it yourself.

The leather curtain at the door was flung back and a biting smell of coal tar poured in. Old Ivan, the railman, stepped over the threshold carrying a storm lantern, hard snow crusting his coat. He spat out a burnt-down cigarette end and stamped his boots on the concrete until it thundered.

Ivan lifted the curtain and went out; the draught turned up one corner of the carbon paper on the desk.

English

The air in Room 602 was a mixture of mildewed plaster, disinfectant, and the ammonia breath of advanced uraemia.

On the iron bed Mama Katya lay under two faded red-and-blue checked wool blankets, the hands left outside the covers mapped with bruise-purple needle marks. Every forty minutes the dialysis machine gave a low, draining suck.

Aleksei crouched beside the bed, wiping his mother's swollen, shiny calves with warm water.

...Alyosha... outside... has the wind stopped?

Not yet, Mama. There's a real smoke-blizzard blowing.

Lana... why isn't Lana off work yet? That child... she went out in thin padded shoes; her heels always get chilblains...

Aleksei's hand faltered; warm water dripped from the cloth into the iron basin with a faint, brittle sound. From his inner coat pocket he drew the crumpled forged note — he had copied his sister's hand two nights before: 'Hospital locked down for two weeks. Staying in the dormitory. Don't worry. Lana.

The railway hospital sent her away on a medical support detail. The telegraph lines are down just now. She got a letter sent: take your medicine on time, she'll be back when the detail ends.

Mama Katya closed her eyes, the sockets sunk in rings of black. She groped for her son's wrist, her rough palm worrying the blue anti-forgery observation stamp on the back of Aleksei's hand.

Alyosha... that blue mark on your hand, it won't wash off, will it? ...Valentina next door says a person stamped like that can't walk at night with his chest held up...

In the corner, the pager terminal sheathed in sheet metal burst into a shrill buzz. At precisely midnight 24:00 its green lamp snapped on, accompanied by the dry, typewriter-like feeding of paper: [Workers' Residential Block 3: today's quotas of necessities and medicine all signed for · Issuing officer: Chief Accountant Dubrov].

In the pipes throughout the building the faint steam whistle gave a puff and stopped. To earn the system's 'whole-building heating rating: compliant,' the accounting office had ticked every undelivered quota as 'signed for.

Aleksei got up and went to the kitchen window. The cracks had been pasted over with three layers of old newspaper and glue, yet the wind still moaned through. Outside, everything was white and empty; two glaring yellow-white searchlight beams crossed slowly over the distant rails.

Back in the entry hall, he pulled on the faded heavy railway greatcoat, tied his trouser cuffs tight with hemp cord, and bent to drag a cast-iron spike crowbar out from under the bed. It was a product of the old locomotive works, heavy in the hand, its prying tip worn snow-bright.

English

Daylight. Rail Cold Storage No.4, north of the city.

Ammonia pipes crisscrossed overhead, thick with white frost; underfoot the floor was crusted with a black hard shell of oil and ice. Aleksei, a conscripted porter, was loading crates of butter and salt fish, sealed with Ministry of Internal Affairs strips, onto a flat handcart.

Behind concrete column 3 in the south hall, a man in a ragged black padded coat knelt beside a gutter of ice water, holding a child tightly wrapped in an old army blanket. The child's face was grey, its lips blue-purple with cold.

The boots of a Ministry patrol stopped on the iron-grate walkway. Sergeant Chernov halted before the column, a portable punch-clock in his hand, its metal probe aimed at the man's hand.

The terminal gave a single beep; a red light blinked.

File 0892, Sergey. Transferred to the Ural Line Station yesterday at 22:00. Central record: cleared. Persons registered at this coordinate: zero.

The man's head snapped up, his eyes threaded with red, his cracked lips split and bleeding.

Officer... the machine's wrong! I never boarded — I was covering a shift at the boiler house yesterday, the child had a fever... I'm not dead, look, Maria's not dead either, she's still breathing!

It says zero up there. The machine reads punch cards. Zero on the register means nobody under this column. Nobody means no ration today.

Chernov reached over and pushed the iron lid of the ration crate shut; the bolt clicked into its slot. The man lunged and clutched the sergeant's trouser leg, his nails scraping a shrill sound across the stiff leather boot.

Officer! One mouthful — just one spoon of hot soup for Maria...

Sergeant Chernov stepped back half a pace. He did not draw his pistol or kick; he only pried the man's fingers loose, one by one, mechanically. As he turned to go, his toe faltered a moment in the snow, kicked aside an empty tin box in the way, and he let fall half a sentence, almost to himself:

...Don't waste time here. Go behind the water tower at West Station, find the cinder-pickers; there might be rotten potatoes. Die in the cold store and not even the corpse cart will come.

The sergeant strode off with his two men.

Aleksei stood three metres away, the handcart's grip creaking under his fingers. In the shadow behind the concrete column, a grey wolfdog with half an ear missing lay on the steps of the abandoned dispatch hut. A length of cut, rusted wire was twisted round its neck; it had once bitten into the flesh and left a ring of scar that would not fade, the fur around it grown thin. It did not go to Sergey, only watched Aleksei quietly.

English

01:30 at night. The curfew siren sounded on schedule, echoing through the whole city.

Aleksei used the crowbar to force the rusted ventilation window of the boiler room on the ground floor below Block 602; the freezing night wind poured straight into his collar. Ash was first through the narrow gap, dropping into the two metres of accumulated snow and flattening warily into a hollow.

Far off, the heavy grinding of rails being stressed came over the tracks. From the top of the tall concrete coal-and-water tower, the yellow-white beam of a Class-I searchlight swept the barbed-wire perimeter at a very slow rhythm.

Aleksei moved along hugging the rusted flank of an abandoned freight car. At a tear in the wire fence ahead, a low moaning suddenly came.

In the reflected glare of the snow Aleksei saw a woman in a grey synthetic coat. The back of her right hand was grown over with coarse grey-white horny scales; the white of her left eye was veiled in a turbid purple-black film — but the film was flaking away at a speed the eye could follow, exposing the dark brown eyeball beneath. She was not attacking the dead things around her; she clutched the iron bars of the stairwell's security door, her knuckles blue with cold.

...Open the door... Kolya, Mummy's fever has broken... I'm not hot any more, the scales on my hand have fallen into the snow... let me see little Anton...

Heavy boots crushed the snow. Two glaring beams caught the woman squarely in their centre.

Two OSK men in lead-grey heavy rubber protective suits came round the culvert, their round pig-snout gas masks throwing off an inorganic, cold sheen in the glare. Infrared sights were mounted under the AKM rifles in their hands; the red dot settled directly on the woman's chest.

The woman flung up a hand against the light, still protesting in vain: 'I'm better... really better, I don't bite...

Pfft. Pfft. Two very short blue flames spat from the suppressed muzzles.

The woman fell at the sound, convulsed twice in the snow, then lay still. Black-red blood soaked into the snow and froze quickly into hard black ice. One of the men, face void of expression, stepped up and kicked the bunch of household copper keys from her hand into the gutter, then worked the teletype terminal at his chest: [One Rabid sighted on the edge of the Red Zone; cleared according to protocol].

The scales on the back of the woman's hand flaked off one by one into the melting snow.

She was not mad. Her fever had broken.

Those who survive the high fever get better.

假人哨塔与新兵帕沙1-4 / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 越过第二道铁轨阻拦索，前方出现了一座三米高的木架哨塔。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 哨塔上伫立着一名端枪的清杀队员，在探照灯的背光下显得异常高大威严。然而灰烬甚至没有压低身子，只是慢吞吞地从塔下走过。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢贴近铁梯。头顶上传来断续的录音机杂音，一台挂在木栏杆上的开盘机正转动着磁带，用失真的大喇叭嗓音反复播报着《告居民书》。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 塔上的“哨兵”是用三根杉木条和旧防化服扎起来的假人，空荡荡的风镜里塞着碎棉花。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 而在铁梯底下的避风背阴处，蜷缩着一个真正的人。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 那是个年轻得有些过分的士兵，防化服套在他身上显得过于宽大，下摆拖在雪地里。他把防毒面具摘了下来扔在脚边，露出一张被高烧烧得干红的脸，嘴唇起了一层白皮。他手里握着一支玻璃水银体温计，借着雪光反复看着刻度：39.2℃。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 听见雪地的碎响，士兵吓得一哆嗦，下意识地去抓地上的步枪，但手抖得连枪带都没握住。

帕沙 （带着浓重的农村口音，吸着鼻涕） ……别……别过来！我手里有家伙……

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢停在三步外，手里的撬棍微微下垂。

阿列克谢 你的枪栓都没拉开。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 帕沙愣了一下，低头看了看枪，眼泪突然涌了出来。他吸了吸鼻子，用冻裂的袖口去擦脸。

帕沙 哥……我求你了，你有退烧药吗？阿司匹林、安乃近，什么都行……我体温到了三十九度二。明天清早督察队要用红外枪量额头，要是过了三十八度五，假人哨塔顶上挂的就该是我了……

阿列克谢 （盯着他身上那件铅灰色的正规军大衣） 你是内务部的人。你们车队里连阿司匹林都没有？

帕沙 （蹲在地上，抱住膝盖发抖） 哪有我们的份啊……那是特勤连的官老爷才有的。我们这种从奥廖尔招来的新兵，发一身大衣服就往铁道上一扔……以前在村里，天旱了怕颗粒无收，涝了怕塌房。穿上这身皮，站在那儿跟木头人一样，队里就管饭，没人问你家里还有几口人。阿廖沙……我听他们这么叫你。当木头人多省心啊，站在塔上一动不动，没人扣你的工分，也没人查你体温……可我一发烧，我就怕……我想我娘腌的酸黄瓜……

English

Past the second rail cable barrier, a three-metre wooden watchtower rose ahead.

On the tower stood an OSK guard with a rifle at the ready, looming unnaturally tall and solemn against the searchlight. Ash did not even lower its body; it merely ambled past beneath.

Aleksei pressed close to the iron ladder. Overhead came the broken crackle of a tape recorder; a reel-to-reel set hung on the wooden rail, its tape turning, repeating a distorted loudspeaker voice reading the 'Notice to Residents'.

The 'sentry' on the tower was a dummy lashed together from three fir planks and an old protective suit, bits of cotton wadding stuffed into the empty goggles.

But at the foot of the ladder, in the windless shade, a real person lay curled up.

He was a soldier ridiculously young, the protective suit far too big for him, its hem trailing in the snow. He had pulled off his gas mask and tossed it by his feet, showing a face flushed dry-red with fever, a white film peeling on his lips. In his hand he held a glass mercury thermometer, and by the light of the snow he read the scale again and again: 39.2°C.

Hearing the crunch of snow, the soldier flinched and reached instinctively for the rifle on the ground, but his hands shook so badly he could not even catch the sling.

...Don't... don't come closer! I've got a gun here...

Aleksei stopped three paces away, the crowbar dipping slightly in his hand.

You haven't even racked the bolt.

Pasha stared, looked down at the rifle, and the tears came suddenly. He sniffed and wiped his face with a cracked, frozen cuff.

Brother... please, do you have anything to bring a fever down? Aspirin, analgin, anything... my temperature's 39.2. At dawn the inspectors will measure foreheads with an infrared gun; if it's over 38.5, I'm the one they'll hang on top of that dummy tower...

You're with the Ministry. Your whole convoy hasn't got a single aspirin?

Not for the likes of us... that's for the gentlemen in the special details. New conscripts like me, dragged in from Oryol — they hand you a big coat and drop you by the railway... Back in the village, in a drought you feared the harvest would fail; in flood you feared the roof would fall. In this skin, you stand there like a wooden man and the unit feeds you; nobody asks how many are left at home. Alyosha... that's what I heard them call you. It's easy being a wooden man — stand on the tower without moving, nobody docks your work points, nobody checks your temperature... but once I start burning, I get scared... I want my mother's pickled cucumbers...

**【抉择 A′ · 帕沙的 39 度 2】** - **A′1（给出退烧片）**：阿列克谢从怀里掏出一片退烧药，递给帕沙。帕沙像接圣餐一样把药片吞了下去，连雪水都没来得及嚼；他缩回袖口的手终于不再发抖。（**良知▲**，帕沙记住了这份药。） - **A′2（绕开不理）**：阿列克谢看了看他，一言不发地退回阴影中。十分钟后，身后传来换岗巡逻队的脚步声，接着是一声沉闷的微声枪响。木头人多了一个，省心的人少了一个。 - **A′3（夺取补给）**：阿列克谢看着他身上的内务部制服，冷声说：“内务部的狗不值得救。你替他们守塔，也算帮凶。”他用撬棍顶住少年的喉咙，扒下备用滤毒罐和口袋里仅剩的半条压缩饼干。帕沙瘫坐在雪里，没再求药。工作日志背面，阿列克谢用钢笔划下第一道深重的竖痕：“|”。

货场黑账与扎哈尔1-4A / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 货场换岗时，阿列克谢从煤水塔后绕过一排待报废的配给箱。调度箱背面贴着一张受潮的编组单：「铁壳号 · 第 7 次特别运输 · 第三节闷罐 · 档案 0401—0576」。0420 在这个区间里。终到栏只印了「乌拉尔山线 · 销账点」。一个戴蓝袖章的核算员正把没收来的手套、滤芯和烟草重新分堆，手里那本账簿没有一页写着失主的名字。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢踩响一块冻裂的冰壳。扎哈尔猛地抬头，正往棉袄里塞的滤芯掉在雪上。远处巡逻灯扫过来；他看了阿列克谢一眼，脸上的血色退了个干净。

核算员扎哈尔 （把账本紧紧按在身前，目光不时扫向巡逻灯） 别喊巡逻队。我叫扎哈尔。以前我替配给站登记每一袋粮，后来那本账上只剩空格。你不把这只滤芯的事报上去，我就跟你走，替你认废料、找补给。别把这当忠诚——我只是不想再挨饿。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢问他，如果清杀队愿意拿配额换一份举报，他会怎么做。扎哈尔没有否认，只用拇指擦掉账本封面上的冻泥。

核算员扎哈尔 要是他们给的配额比你给的多，我会把知道的都报上去。账不会替谁讲情。

English

During shift change, Aleksei skirts the scrap crates. A damp consist sheet lists Buran, Special Run 7, Boxcar Three, Files 0401–0576. That includes 0420. Destination: Urals Line · Closure Point. Nearby a blue-armband accountant sorts confiscated gloves, filters and tobacco. His ledger has no names.

Aleksei's boot crunches on a patch of frozen ice. Zakhar jerks his head up; the filter cartridge he was slipping into his coat falls into the snow. A patrol light sweeps across the yard. He looks at Aleksei, and the color drains from his face.

Don't call the patrol. My name is Zakhar. I used to record every sack at the ration office; now that ledger holds nothing but blanks. If you don't report this filter, I'll come with you and help identify salvage and find supplies. Don't mistake it for loyalty. I just don't want to go hungry again.

Aleksei asked what Zakhar would do if the OSK offered him a ration quota in exchange for a denunciation. Zakhar did not deny it; he only wiped the frozen mud from the ledger's cover with his thumb.

If they offer me a larger quota than you do, I'll report everything I know. A ledger pleads for no one.

白桦林公寓东窗与 601 室的算计2-1 / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 白桦林公寓原本是一栋纺织女工宿舍，如今整栋楼被四米高的铁丝网和铁皮封板包裹得像一只铁皮棺材。一楼的所有门窗全被厚钢板封死，只在东侧留了一个半米见方的铁抽屉窗口，用于每日下午四点前进行物资交接。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 15:52。阿列克谢抱着一个用粗布包裹的铁皮奶粉罐冲过铁轨下穿涵洞。那是他在西区旧仓库翻出的深度水解抗敏奶粉，罐身生了锈，但密封完好。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 东侧交接窗口前，负责消毒的蓝袖章核算员正拿着一把长嘴喷壶，对着铁抽屉喷洒浓烈的来苏尔药水。

核算员 系统词条里只有标准 1 号全脂奶粉，你这个是私货，没有检疫铅封。按规程，就地销毁。

阿列克谢 （一把按住下坠的铁闸门） 402 的孩子吃全脂奶粉喉咙水肿快憋死了！你睁开眼看看，这是药房的封条！

核算员 我只看终端。四点整主脑断网平账，多一件无码物品，整栋楼扣除两分信用值。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 15:59。电动铁闸发出低沉的下压齿轮咬合声。阿列克谢额头青筋暴起，直接将手里的道钉撬棍横着卡进了齿轮槽！火花四溅中，钢铁挤压出令人牙酸的摩擦声。他猛地拉开抽屉，将奶粉罐顺着滑道狠狠推了进去！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 铁窗内侧，一只布满冻疮的年轻女人的手一把抱住了奶粉罐，窗缝里传来 402 室莉莉娅带着哭腔的抽泣：“……谢谢……谢谢……”

旁白 / 阿列克谢 然而，当阿列克谢拔出撬棍跌坐在雪地里时，他抬起头，看见对面三楼、四楼好几个被霜花封住的玻璃窗后面，有几双眼睛正冷冷地盯着他。一台老式胶卷相机的镜头在窗帘缝隙里闪了一下。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 当夜。601 室门外。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢顺着楼梯间上楼时，看见 601 室的房门开了一条缝。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 六十三岁的柳德米拉大娘正坐在门口的小马扎上，借着楼道里昏暗的应急灯，手里反复数着两张红色的硬纸片。那是区管委新推行的“加配领粮券”，每张券可以换半斤黑麦粉。纸片边缘沾着几滴已经干涸的红漆。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 五岁的外孙女佳娜趴在大娘膝盖上，穿着一件极不合身的旧棉袍，正吸吮着一根发黑的空木勺。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢在楼梯缓台上停住脚。他认出了那张券——今天下午举报 402 室私收走私货的举报箱里，就插着这种信封。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 柳德米拉大娘听见了脚步声，手一抖，两张券落在了地上。她看清了是阿列克谢，眼神先是一慌，随即迅速板了起来，像一层冻硬了的水泥。

柳德米拉大娘 （把地上的券捡起来，用袖子蹭干净上面的灰） 看什么看？阿廖沙，你别用那种眼神看我。我没偷没抢。

阿列克谢 （扶了扶眼镜，声音低沉） 402 楼道门刚才被人泼了红漆，莉莉娅连水房都出不去了。

柳德米拉大娘 （声音猛地拔高，随即又心虚地压低，喉咙里发出粗重的倒气声） 那人昨天还帮我修过门轴，今天就被拖进冷库了！可我不写那张条子，佳娜拿什么冲奶？你们谁管过她哭？你妈在六楼好歹有透析液挂着，我外孙女两天没屙出一粒屎，肚子里全咽的是被面上的棉花絮！那两罐洋奶粉是她该得的吗？大家都在喝发霉的黑麦糊，凭什么她家孩子能吃洋粉？我给管委写条子，我换了两斤黑麦面，佳娜今晚能喝上一口稠的……我错哪儿了？你说啊，我错哪儿了？！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 佳娜被姥姥的喊声吓着了，把小脸埋进大娘怀里，小声嘟囔：“姥姥，不吵……佳娜不饿了……”

旁白 / 阿列克谢 柳德米拉紧紧搂住孩子，眼泪顺着满是褶子的脸颊淌进嘴角的苦纹里。她没有再说一句话，只是砰地一声把防盗门摔上，铁栓咔哒一声反锁了。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢站在昏暗的楼道里，楼下穿堂风从破损的玻璃缝里吹进来，吹得地上一张废弃的公文纸在水泥地上沙沙作响。

English

Birch Grove Apartments was once a dormitory for female textile workers; now the whole building is wrapped in four-metre wire mesh and sheet-metal panels like a tin coffin. Every door and window on the ground floor is sealed shut with thick steel plate, save for a half-metre-square iron drawer-hatch on the east side, used for the daily handover of supplies before four o'clock each afternoon.

15:52. Aleksei sprints through the railway underpass, clutching a tin of formula wrapped in coarse cloth. He found it in an old West District warehouse — extensively hydrolyzed hypoallergenic formula, the tin rusted but the seal intact.

At the east handover hatch, the blue-armband clerk in charge of disinfection is spraying the iron drawer with a long-spouted can of strong Lysol solution.

The system registry lists only Standard No. 1 whole-milk formula. What you have is private stock, with no quarantine seal. By regulation, it must be destroyed on the spot.

The child in Room 402 is choking — his throat is swelling from whole-milk formula! Open your eyes and look: this is a pharmacy seal!

I only read the terminal. At four the Central Mainframe goes offline to balance the books; one unmarked item too many costs the whole building two credit points.

15:59. The electric shutter grinds down with a low meshing of gears. The veins bulge on Aleksei's forehead as he jams his railway-spike crowbar sideways into the gear track. Sparks fly; steel grinds against steel with a sound that sets the teeth on edge. He wrenches the drawer open and shoves the tin hard down the chute.

Inside the iron hatch, a young woman's hand, covered in chilblains, closes around the tin. Through the gap comes the sobbing voice of Lilia in Room 402: “...Thank you... thank you...”

Yet when Aleksei pulls the crowbar free and drops into the snow, he looks up and sees, behind several frost-sealed windows on the third and fourth floors across the way, pairs of eyes fixed coldly upon him. The lens of an old film camera glints in a gap in the curtains.

That night. Outside Room 601.

As Aleksei climbs the stairwell, he sees the door of Room 601 standing ajar.

Sixty-three-year-old Grandmother Lyudmila sits on a small folding stool by the door, counting the same two red cardboard slips over and over in the dim emergency light. They are the District Rationing Committee's new “supplemental ration coupons,” each worth half a jin of rye flour. Dried drops of red paint cling to their edges.

Her five-year-old granddaughter Dyana lies across her knees in a badly ill-fitting old padded coat, sucking on a blackened empty wooden spoon.

Aleksei stops on the landing. He recognizes the coupons — the denunciation box this afternoon, for the smuggled goods taken in by Room 402, held envelopes just like these.

Lyudmila hears his footsteps; her hand jerks, and the two slips fall to the floor. When she makes out Aleksei, her expression falters for an instant, then hardens — like a layer of frozen cement.

What are you staring at? Don't look at me like that, Alyosha. I haven't stolen anything, I haven't robbed anyone.

Someone threw red paint over the door to Room 402 just now. Lilia can't even get out to the washroom.

That man fixed my door hinge only yesterday, and today they dragged him off to cold storage! But if I don't write that note, what will Dyana use to mix her milk? Which of you ever cared when she cried? Your mother at least has dialysis fluid running on the sixth floor; my granddaughter hasn't passed a thing in two days — all that's in her belly is cotton wadding off the quilt! Those two tins of foreign formula — did she deserve them? Everyone's drinking mouldy rye gruel; why should that family's child get foreign powder? I wrote the Committee a note, I got two jin of rye flour, and tonight Dyana can have one thick mouthful... Where did I go wrong? Tell me — where did I go wrong?!

Frightened by Granny's shouting, Dyana buries her little face in her grandmother's bosom and mutters softly: “Granny, don't fight... Dyana isn't hungry anymore...”

Lyudmila holds the child tight, tears running down her lined cheeks into the bitter creases about her mouth. She says not another word; she only slams the security door shut with a bang, and the iron bolt clicks locked.

Aleksei stands in the dim corridor. From below, a draught pushes through the broken glass and sets a discarded official paper rustling across the concrete floor.

后厨的砍骨刀与瓦西里的“渴”2-2 / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 东街工人食堂。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 这里早在一个月前就停止了集中供应，整栋建筑被列入“账面清空区”。但阿列克谢知道，老炊事员费奥多尔依然躲在后厨，守着地窖里仅剩的三袋陈化黑麦粒，偷偷给附近滞留的十九个老铁路工熬糊糊。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 后厨弥漫着酸败的油脂味和发馊的面粉气味。地面积着寸许厚的黑水，水面上浮着薄冰。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢挑开破烂的布帘跨进后厨。灶台边，费奥多尔老爹正蹲在地上用小煤炉煮雪水，磨旧的袖口沾着炉灰。

费奥多尔 （抬头看了看阿列克谢） 阿廖沙……你来得不巧。锅里只有雪水。地窖快见底了。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 话音未落，储藏室那扇用粗铁丝捆着的木门突然发出了一声沉重的闷响。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 嘭！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 整扇木门被一股巨大的力量撞得向外凸起，木屑崩落。费奥多尔手里的铁勺当啷一声掉进水盆里，脸色刷地惨白。

费奥多尔 （声音剧烈发抖） 瓦西里……是我儿子！他前天夜里冒着大雪去给四楼瘫痪的老钳工送糊糊，半路上冻透了，回来就发起高烧……我把他锁在里头，给他灌了姜水，可他……他手上的皮全硬了……

旁白 / 阿列克谢 木门在第二下撞击中彻底炸裂开来！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 一个二十出头的小伙子从黑暗中跌撞着冲了出来。他浑身滚烫，呼出的白气带着刺鼻的酸臭味，右手背到小臂覆盖着一层厚厚的灰白角质硬鳞。他的双眼布满紫黑色的血翳，喉咙里发出类似风箱漏气般的急促喘息，毫无目标地挥舞着双手，一头撞翻了备餐的铁案台！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 案台上的大铁盆砸在地上，发出震耳欲聋的巨响。瓦西里被巨响刺激，猛地扭过头，浑浊的眼睛锁定了站在门口的阿列克谢，嘶吼着扑了过来！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢被撞得倒退数步，后背狠狠砸在冷藏柜锋利的铁角上，剧痛让他眼前一黑。瓦西里的力气大得惊人，滚烫的双手死死掐住了阿列克谢的脖子，口水和带血的泡沫滴在阿列克谢的镜片上。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 空气被一点点抽离。阿列克谢的右手在油腻的砧板上胡乱摸索，手指触到了一个冰冷沉重的木柄——那是费奥多尔砍冻猪肉用的宽背砍骨刀。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 生死只在一瞬。恐惧彻底压垮了理智。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢猛地抽出砍骨刀，借着下坠的力量，自下而上用尽全身力气劈了出去！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 咔嚓！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 厚重的生锈刀刃准确地楔进了瓦西里的左侧锁骨窝，深陷进骨缝之中。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 扑击的力量戛然而止。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 滚烫的血像打开的阀门一样喷涌而出，溅了阿列克谢满脸。瓦西里喉咙里的嘶吼停住了。随着大量鲜血的流失，他眼眶里那层紫黑色的血翳像是退潮一般迅速消散，露出了底下原本清澈明亮的浅蓝色眼珠。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 那个狂暴的“怪物”在两秒钟之内消失了。靠在阿列克谢肩头的，变成了一个刚满二十岁、眼神里充满惊恐与茫然的年轻木讷工棚小伙。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 瓦西里看着阿列克谢，嘴唇剧烈颤抖着，喉咙里咕噜作响，用尽最后的力气吐出了一个含混不清的俄语词汇：

瓦西里 （眼神涣散，瞳孔放大） ……Пить……（水……渴……）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 他的头垂了下来，重重地砸在阿列克谢的肩膀上，再也没有动弹。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢双手握着刀柄，浑身筛糠般发抖。他想把刀拔出来，但刀刃被锁骨死死卡住，稍一用力，就发出令人骨髓发冷的骨头摩擦声。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 费奥多尔跌跌撞撞地爬过来，噗通一声跪在儿子身边。老人没有扑上来打阿列克谢，只是伸出颤抖的双手，摸了摸儿子渐渐冰凉的脸颊。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢落下了终生无法痊愈的心理创伤——创伤性干渴耳鸣。每当他在极度紧张或深夜潜行时，耳边便会反复轰鸣着骨裂声，以及那个在血泊中微弱求饶的词句：“Пить……”

English

East Street Workers' Canteen.

Centralized food distribution here stopped a full month ago; the whole building was marked a “ledger-cleared zone.” But Aleksei knows the one-eyed old cook Fyodor is still hiding in the kitchen, guarding the last three sacks of aged rye in the cellar and quietly boiling gruel for the nineteen old railway workers stranded nearby.

The kitchen reeks of rancid fat and sour flour. An inch of black water lies on the floor, with a skin of thin ice floating on it.

Aleksei pushes aside the ragged cloth curtain and steps into the kitchen. By the stove, old Fyodor crouches on the floor, boiling snow water over a small coal brazier, the black leather patch over his one eye worn shiny.

Alyosha... you've come at a bad time. There's only snow water in the pot. The cellar's nearly empty.

Before the words are out, the storeroom door — a plank bound with coarse iron wire — gives a heavy, muffled thud.

Bang!

The whole door bows outward under some tremendous force, splinters flying. The iron ladle clatters out of Fyodor's hand into the washbasin, and his face goes white in an instant.

Vasily... he's my son! The night before last he went out in the blizzard to take gruel to the paralysed old fitter on the fourth floor, and was frozen through before he got back — then the fever took him... I locked him in and gave him ginger water, but his... the skin on his hands has all gone hard...

The door bursts completely apart on the second impact!

A young man, barely twenty, staggers out of the darkness. He is burning hot; the white breath from his mouth carries a sharp, sour stench. His right hand and forearm are covered in a thick layer of grey-white keratinous scale. Purple-black clouds fill his eyes, and a rapid, bellows-leaking rasp works in his throat. He flails blindly and crashes headlong into the steel prep table, upsetting it.

The great iron basin on the table hits the floor with a deafening clang. The noise jolts Vasily: his head snaps round, his clouded eyes fix on Aleksei in the doorway, and he lunges with a shriek.

The blow drives Aleksei back several steps; his spine strikes the sharp iron corner of the cold-storage cabinet, and the pain blacks out his sight. Vasily's strength is terrifying. His scalding hands lock around Aleksei's throat, and spittle and bloody foam drip onto the lenses of his glasses.

The air is drawn out of him bit by bit. Aleksei's right hand gropes blindly over the greasy chopping block, and his fingers close on a cold, heavy wooden handle — Fyodor's broad-backed cleaver for frozen pork.

Life and death turn on a single instant. Fear breaks reason utterly.

Aleksei snatches the cleaver free and, riding the downward pull, drives it upward with all the strength in his body.

Crack!

The heavy, rusted blade wedges true into the hollow above Vasily's left collarbone, sinking deep between the bones.

The force of the lunge stops dead.

Scalding blood gushes out as though a valve had been opened, spattering Aleksei's face. The rasp in Vasily's throat ceases. As the blood drains away, the purple-black film in his eyes recedes like an ebbing tide, laying bare the clear, bright pale-blue eyes beneath.

The Rabid “monster” is gone within two seconds. What slumps against Aleksei's shoulder is a slow, gangly young bunkhouse lad just turned twenty, his eyes full of terror and bewilderment.

Vasily looks at Aleksei, lips shuddering, throat gurgling, and with his last strength forces out one slurred Russian word:

“...Пить... (Water... thirsty...)”

His head drops forward, lands heavily on Aleksei's shoulder, and never moves again.

Aleksei grips the handle in both hands, shaking like a leaf in a gale. He tries to pull the blade out, but the collarbone holds it fast; the least effort brings a grating of bone that chills the marrow.

Fyodor stumbles over and drops with a thud to his knees beside his son. He does not throw himself at Aleksei; he only reaches out with trembling hands and touches his son's cooling cheek.

Aleksei is left with a psychological wound that will never heal — PTSD Thirst Tinnitus. Whenever he is under extreme strain or moving in the small hours, his ears ring again with the crack of splitting bone, and with that faint word of pleading from the pool of blood: “Пить...”

回访后厨与背负的权利2-3 / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 两天后的深夜。气温降至零下三十四度。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢顶着高烧，独自一人重新回到了东街工人食堂的后厨。他的棉大衣内侧藏着他在黑市用两瓶高纯度医用酒精换来的三小袋黑麦粉。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 后厨的水已经冻成了结实的黑冰。费奥多尔老爹坐在灶台旁，身上披着一件旧羊皮袄。瓦西里的遗体已经被抬到了案台上，盖着一条洗得发白的粗帆布，露出一双穿着破胶鞋的脚。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢走到老人面前，两腿一软，跪在满是凝固血污的冰面上。

阿列克谢 （低下头，把装面粉的布袋推到老人脚边，声音沙哑得几乎听不清） 费奥多尔大叔……打死我吧。瓦西里冲出来的时候脚底下踩滑了……我手里握着撬棍，我本可以砸他的膝盖……可我害怕了，我挑了最省事的砍骨刀。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 老人很久没有出声。屋子里只有北风灌进烟囱的呜咽声。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 过了很久，一只粗糙、布满烫伤硬茧的大手落在了阿列克谢被雪打湿的头顶上。老人没有拿刀，也没有把面粉扔出去。他站起身，走到瓦西里的遗体旁，从旁边叠着的衣服堆里拿起了一件带补丁的深灰色厚棉坎肩。那是瓦西里生前干活时常穿的。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 费奥多尔把坎肩披在了阿列克谢不住发抖的肩膀上。

费奥多尔 他临死前喊渴，你记着这声渴就行。别拿‘他是疯子’来安慰自己，那是我儿子。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 老人指了指阿列克谢怀里露出一角的工作日志。

费奥多尔 他叫瓦西里·费奥多罗维奇。把名字、年纪和工种都记上：二十岁，机务段的三级司炉工。等全城的门砸开那天，你当着大伙的面，把他的名字念出来。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢翻开工作日志，在背面一笔一划地写下瓦西里的全名。灶台后响了两下钉锤。一个提着帆布工具包的老人直起身，把没用完的钉子拢进口袋。他一直在帮费奥多尔钉瓦西里的木箱。

帕维尔老爹 我叫帕维尔，在机务段修了一辈子车门。费奥多尔把儿子的坎肩给了你，我看见了。下回碰上焊死的门，叫上我。你那根撬棍，得留着接人。

English

Late at night, two days later. The temperature has fallen to minus thirty-four.

Running a high fever, Aleksei returns alone to the kitchen of the East Street Workers' Canteen. Inside his padded coat he carries three small bags of rye flour, traded on the black market for two bottles of high-purity medical alcohol.

The water on the kitchen floor has frozen into solid black ice. Old Fyodor sits beside the stove in an old sheepskin coat. Vasily's body has been laid out on the table under a faded coarse canvas, leaving a pair of feet in broken rubber shoes exposed.

Aleksei comes up to the old man; his legs give way, and he kneels on the ice crusted over with dried blood.

Uncle Fyodor... beat me to death. Vasily slipped as he came at me... I had the crowbar in my hand; I could have smashed his knees... but I was afraid. I took the easy way — the cleaver.

For a long time the old man says nothing. The only sound in the room is the north wind whining down the chimney.

After a long while, a rough hand, calloused with old burns, settles on Aleksei's snow-wet head. The old man takes up no knife, nor does he fling the flour away. He rises, walks over to Vasily's body, and from the pile of folded clothes beside it takes a patched, dark-grey, thickly padded vest — the one Vasily used to wear for his work.

Fyodor lays the vest over Aleksei's ceaselessly trembling shoulders.

He cried out for water as he died. Just hold on to that thirst. Don't comfort yourself with “he had gone mad.” He was my son.

The old man points at the corner of the work logbook showing inside Aleksei's coat.

His name was Vasily Fyodorovich. Write down his name, age, and trade: twenty years old, a third-grade stoker in the locomotive depot. When the doors across the city finally come down, read his name aloud in front of everyone.

旧疗养院与《回退手稿》2-4 / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 旧传染病疗养院地下二层。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 这里曾经是结核病隔离病区，阴暗潮湿的水泥墙上结满了白色的硝霜。空气中弥漫着高浓度石炭酸与腐烂组织的怪味。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢穿过堆满铁架床的走廊，在一间焊着厚铁栏杆的配药室里见到了前铁路医院副院长梁诺夫医生，以及他的学生、流调员古丽娅。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 在靠墙的一张铁床上，用医用牛皮带死死绑着一个年轻女孩。她在高烧中昏睡了三天，几乎没有醒来，手腕被皮带磨得皮开肉绽。但此刻，她身上的高热正在消退，原本硬化如蛇皮的灰白角质正大片大片地从胸口和颈部剥落，露出底下粉红色的新生皮肤。她的呼吸虚弱而均匀。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 古丽娅医师眼睛通红，手里紧紧攥着三页薄薄的病历纸，纸角浸透了黄褐色的药水污渍。

古丽娅 （把病历纸塞到阿列克谢眼前，手指由于激动而泛白） 第三十七例！阿廖沙，这是第三十七例完整记录！只要给他们水，熬过第四天的高热和抽搐，灰鳞就会开始剥落！这不是绝症，他们能自己挺过来！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢接过那三页《回退临床手稿》。上面的每一行数据、每一个体温曲线，都用蓝黑墨水记录得清清楚楚。但在最后一页的末尾，盖着一个刺眼的红色长方形印章：【退回 · 结论含有动摇防线内容 · 不予归档】。

梁诺夫医生 （坐在药箱上，用止血钳夹着棉球擦拭眼镜） 三天前，我把手稿交给了督察专员。他甚至没翻开第二页，直接当着我的面扔进了碎纸炉。他说：‘梁诺夫同志，布拉风号已经拉走了四千人，乌拉尔山线已经平了四千笔账。如果你的报告是真的，那四千个被销账的人算什么？’

旁白 / 阿列克谢 老医生的手停在半空，眼眶凹陷。

梁诺夫医生 他们不是不知道人能好起来。他们是不能让活着的人好起来。活着的人一退烧，他们手里的枪和单子就全成了杀人罪证。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 铁床上的女孩突然发出一声微弱的呻吟。阿列克谢猛地转过头。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 她艰难地睁开眼睛，瞳孔清澈透明。她看了看阿列克谢胸前的铁路调度工牌，又看向他大衣口袋里露出的妹妹照片，干裂的嘴唇吃力地开合：

安娜 ……照片……0420……是拉娜护士吗？

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢像触电一样冲到铁床边，单膝跪地。

阿列克谢 是我妹妹。你怎么会在疗养院？

安娜 我是安娜，档案号 0419。清杀队来留观站押人时，我烧到休克，被当成死人丢在站台上。梁医生和古丽娅发现我还有气，把我带回疗养院。高烧时我怕伤人，才用皮带把自己绑在床栏上。

阿列克谢 那你最后一次见拉娜时，她还醒着吗？

安娜 昨晚装车前，我短暂醒过一次。拉娜让我再看一眼体温计：三十六度八。她手背的鳞都掉了，还隔着铁网把半片阿司匹林塞给我。她让我记住你在货运站，说你会来接她。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢把照片塞回口袋，几次都没对准袋口。档案上只剩个编号。现在他有个活人要接回来。

English

Old Infectious Disease Sanatorium, second basement level.

This was once a tuberculosis isolation ward; white nitrate frost has bloomed across the dark, damp concrete. The air is heavy with the strange reek of strong carbolic acid and rotting tissue.

Aleksei passes down a corridor heaped with iron-framed beds and finds Dr. Leonov, former deputy director of the railway hospital, together with his student, the epidemiologist Gulya, in a dispensary room behind thick welded iron bars.

On an iron bed against the wall, a young girl is strapped down with medical leather belts. She has spent three days lost in a fevered sleep, barely waking; the straps have rubbed her wrists raw. But now her fever is breaking. Grey-white keratin, hardened like snakeskin, is sloughing from her chest and neck in great sheets, revealing pink new skin beneath. Her breathing is faint but even.

Dr. Gulya's eyes are red-rimmed; she clutches three thin sheets of case notes in her hand, their corners soaked through with yellow-brown medicine stains.

Case thirty-seven! Alyosha, the thirty-seventh complete record! Keep them hydrated and get them through the fever and convulsions on the fourth day; the Grey Scale starts to slough off! This isn't terminal. They can pull through on their own.

Aleksei takes the three pages of the Clinical Regression Manuscript. Every line of data, every temperature curve, is set down plainly in blue-black ink. But at the foot of the last page is a glaring red rectangular stamp: [REJECTED · CONCLUSIONS CONTAIN MATERIAL THAT UNDERMINES THE LINE · NOT TO BE FILED].

Three days ago I handed the manuscript to the inspector. He did not even open the second page — he threw it into the shredder before my eyes. He said: “Comrade Leonov, the armoured train Buran has already carried away four thousand people, and the Ural line has balanced four thousand accounts. If your report is true, what does that make those four thousand written-off souls?”

The old doctor's hand hangs motionless in mid-air; his eyes are sunken in their sockets.

It is not that they don't know people can get better. They cannot allow the living to get better. The moment a living person's fever breaks, the guns and the forms in their hands become evidence of murder.

The girl on the iron bed lets out a faint moan. Aleksei turns sharply toward her.

She opens her eyes with difficulty, her pupils clear. She looks at the railway dispatcher's badge on Aleksei's chest, then at the photograph of his sister peeking from his coat pocket. Her cracked lips move with effort:

“...The photograph... 0420... is that Nurse Lana?”

Aleksei rushes to the iron bed as though struck by lightning and drops to one knee.

She's my sister. How did you get to the sanatorium?

I'm Anna, file 0419. When the OSK came to take people from the observation ward, I went into shock from the fever and collapsed on the platform. They left me there for dead. Dr. Leonov and Gulya found I was still breathing and brought me back to the sanatorium. I was afraid I'd hurt someone in the fever, so I strapped myself to the bed rail.

When you last saw Lana, was she still awake?

Before they loaded her last night, I woke briefly. Lana asked me to check the thermometer once more: thirty-six point eight. The scales had fallen from the back of her hand. She slipped me half an aspirin through the wire. She told me to remember you worked at the freight yard and said you would come for her.

Aleksei tries twice to put the photograph away before finding the pocket. The file held a number. Now he has someone alive to bring home.

**【抉择 B · 手稿的归宿】** - **B1（上交督察组）**：阿列克谢在巨大的恐惧下，把手稿交给了内务巡逻队换取两瓶腹透液。真相被彻底掩埋。日志背面划下血痕“|”。（**良知▼**，真结局关闭）。 - **B2（贴身保存）**：阿列克谢将三页手稿折好，缝进了自己棉大衣内胆贴近心口的位置：“老院长，只要我阿廖沙还有一口气，这几页纸就不会变成灰。”（**真相存活**，**良知▲**）。 - **B3（交老轨伊万）**：阿列克谢带手稿潜往黑市，交给老伊万通过铁路秘密线路翻印扩散。（**真相存活**）。

第八章的分水岭3-1 / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 极夜的寒潮提前降临，室外气温跌破零下三十八度。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 市政集中供暖管道在一声巨响后爆裂，高压蒸汽将整条街笼罩在白茫茫的死雾中，随后彻底冷却。赫鲁晓夫楼的铸铁暖气片在半小时内结了一层厚厚的白霜。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 602 室的透析机由于电压骤降发出尖锐的报警蜂鸣。最后一袋瑞士 409 批腹膜透析液在昨夜用尽。

English

The polar night's cold wave came early; the temperature outside fell below minus thirty-eight degrees.

The municipal heating main burst with a great bang; high-pressure steam shrouded the whole street in a blank white fog of death, then went cold for good. Within half an hour the cast-iron radiators of the Khrushchyovka were coated in a thick white frost.

In Room 602 the dialysis machine shrieked a sharp alarm as the voltage plunged. The last bag of Swiss Batch-409 peritoneal dialysis fluid had run out the night before.

**【剧情分水岭判定：母亲的生死】** - **分支 α（卡佳妈妈存活线）**： 若玩家在前七章成功保住了至少三袋透析液并维持了室内临时煤炉供暖。阿列克谢在凌晨将换来的透析液接上管道。卡佳妈妈虽然极度虚弱，但呼吸逐渐平稳。她握住儿子的手：“去吧，阿廖沙……去把拉娜找回来……妈看着炉子，妈等你。”阿列克谢将家门钥匙贴身收好，转身踏入风雪。（进入**家园线**）。 - **分支 β（卡佳妈妈去世 · 失母荒原线）**： 若透析液断供超过四十八小时。当阿列克谢推开门时，透析机已经显示长鸣的红字。床上的母亲蜷缩成一团，体重在尿毒症与极寒中消耗得只剩下七十二斤。卡佳妈妈在临终前拔掉了腹部的插管，把那床旧羊毛毯整整齐齐地叠在床头，留下一张被铅笔字擦花的硬纸片：*“阿廖沙，别换药了，换车票。带拉娜去南边。”* 阿列克谢在冻得如铁板一样的床前跪了整整两个小时。他没有哭，只是默默脱下大衣，将母亲抱下六楼，安放在废弃洗车库避风的雪坑里。他锁好 602，把钥匙放回大衣内袋。黑市废信号室里，他把它攥了一夜。 **阿列克谢** 我会回来。妈妈的围巾还在床边，拉娜的事也还没完。先活过今晚。

黑市暗号与老轨的路线图3-2 / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 午夜。废弃机车调车库。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 这里是诺沃斯克-17 最后的地下黑市。废弃机车库的铁轨间挤着帆布棚、补锅摊和换药的柜台；炉火把棚顶熏黑，几处灯还亮着，摊主敲打零件的声音一直没停。清杀队的巡逻车只在外围绕行——棚户巷窄，废车和岔道多，灯一照进去，反倒成了私兵的靶场。几名拿旧步枪和道钉枪的守卫看住出入口，狂暴者闯近时，他们就鸣枪把人赶回铁轨另一侧。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 这里谈不上富足，但比配给站更像一个还在运转的地方：有人修鞋、有人磨刀，也有人拿半袋煤换一把抗生素。被户籍簿和配给表一笔划掉的人，都挤在这片黑区里，靠交换、修补和彼此守夜撑下去。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 几个药贩子围在一台老式矿石收音机旁。短波 6.66 MHz 上反复传出黑市商贩的买卖暗号：【六六六……土豆换鳞粉……烙铁保平安……】那是摊主招揽生意的呼号，不是求救信号。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 在车厢暗处，一个假药贩正将一根烧得通红的火烙铁按在一个工人的手背上。焦糊的人肉臭味伴随着惨叫声散开，工人原本轻微起鳞的手背被硬生生烫成一片焦黑的烂肉。

假药贩 （收走工人的手表，冷笑） 喊什么喊！烫成三级烧伤，清杀队的刮片枪就测不出反应！这就叫‘工业事故’，懂不懂？不然明早直接拉上铁壳号！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢挑开帘子。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 > 灰烬同行　灰烬从他靴边冲出去，露出犬齿。药贩退到煤堆后面。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 > 灰烬缺席　阿列克谢用撬棍敲响铁皮桶。烙铁掉进雪泥，腾起一缕白汽。药贩退到煤堆后面。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 机车驾驶室里，老轨伊万正坐在悬空的铁座上，就着一听发绿的腌酸黄瓜喝着烈性工业酒精。

老轨伊万 （把一张浸透机油的路线纸拍在仪表盘上） 小子，内务部特勤连押的是一百七十六个退了烧的人。疗养所是写给家属看的。铁壳号真要去鹰嘴崖 3 号废矿。列车一进矿区，他们就封死洞口，把人和账一道抹掉。发车时刻我不知道，只有西站调度室那道盖红章的令上有。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢摊平路线纸。编组单上那行「销账点」忽然有了一个地名。他把指尖按在矿坑口，正好盖住那一小块黑墨。

阿列克谢 你怎么混进编组站的？

老轨伊万 （吐了口浓痰） 我在这条山线上开了二十五年。西站有张检修图，抄出来。车真停在鹰嘴崖，你得先知道从哪儿下到谷底。还有，市场东边那间旧信号检修室，我在 4.17 MHz 上听到过几秒短波回声。清杀队的监听组正在找这段频率，发报的人应该还在附近。

English

Midnight. The abandoned locomotive roundhouse.

This is the last underground market in Novorsk-17. Canvas stalls, repair benches, and medicine counters crowd the tracks between abandoned locomotives. Stove smoke blackens the roofs; a few lamps still burn, and stallholders keep hammering at parts. OSK patrol trucks circle the perimeter but do not enter the narrow lanes and branching rows of derelict cars. Armed guards with old rifles and rail spikes watch the entrances, driving Rabid back across the tracks when they come near.

There is no real abundance here, but the place feels more alive than the ration office: someone repairs boots, someone sharpens knives, someone trades half a sack of coal for antibiotics. People crossed out of the census and ration ledgers crowd into this black zone, surviving by barter, repairs, and keeping watch for one another.

A few medicine sellers crowd around an old crystal radio. On shortwave 6.66 MHz, the black-market traders repeat their barter code: [Six-six-six... potatoes for scale-powder... the branding iron keeps you safe...]. It is a call for business, not a distress signal.

In the dark of a boxcar, a fake-medicine peddler was pressing a red-hot branding iron against a worker's hand. The reek of scorched flesh spread with the man's screams; the back of his hand, where the scales had only just begun to show, was seared into a charred mass of raw meat.

What are you yelling about? Burned that badly, their scraper won't register anything. That's an ‘industrial accident,’ understand? Otherwise, they'll put you straight on the Buran in the morning!

Aleksei lifts the curtain.

If Ash is present, the dog rushes out and bares his teeth. The vendor backs behind the coal pile.

Without Ash, Aleksei strikes a tin drum with his crowbar. The iron lands in the slush and the vendor backs behind the coal pile.

In the locomotive cab, Old Ivan sat on a hanging iron seat, drinking potent industrial alcohol and chasing it with a tin of green pickles.

Kid, the OSK special unit is escorting a hundred and seventy-six people whose fevers have broken. The sanatorium is what they tell the families. The Buran is really headed to the abandoned Pit No. 3 at Eagle's Crag. Once the train enters the mine, they will seal the entrance and erase the people along with the records. I don't know the departure time; only the red-stamped order in the West Station dispatch room has it.

Aleksei spreads out the route sheet. The closure point on the consist has a name now. His fingertip covers the little black mark at the mouth of the pit.

How did you get into the marshalling yard?

I've driven this mountain line for twenty-five years. I copied a maintenance map from West Station. If the train stops at Eagle's Crag, you need to know how to get down to the valley. There's something else: I heard a few seconds of shortwave echo at 4.17 MHz from the old signal workshop east of the market. The OSK listening unit is tracking that frequency. Whoever sent it should still be nearby.

4.17 兆赫与旧信号检修室3-2A / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 市场地图东侧棚户巷标着一间旧铁路信号检修室。阿列克谢推开门时，收音机的 6.66 MHz 买卖暗号已经听不见了；桌上的短波机却刚从 4.17 MHz 切回静音。电池、手摇发电柄和压在旋钮下的庄园平面图说明，这个固定发射点已用了很久。内务部的监听组也在追这段频率，附近每多响几秒，搜查圈就会收紧一圈。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 门后的女人没有举枪。她先关掉发射机，再把监听耳机摘下来。她是茹科夫专员的女儿叶莲娜，也是这条秘密广播的架设者。她用低功率的 4.17 MHz 短波，断续向城外的医疗救援频道通报退烧者仍然活着；内务部正沿着这段频率测向搜查。她把发射点固定在这间旧信号检修室，也保留了一条从排污检修管进入松林庄园的路线。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 叶莲娜留着金色长发，穿着剪裁合身、面料厚实的冬季外套，领口的毛边和袖口都打理得干净。她看起来比外面的人体面许多，但脸上没有庄园里那种安稳。

叶莲娜 （把频率表推到桌子中央） 我播了很多次。终于有人回话，我却先把发射机关了。我父亲把账本和第三节的记录锁在庄园里。排污管格栅松了，我带你进去。

阿列克谢 你知道他们已经在追这段频率了吗？

叶莲娜 知道。天线我已经收了。先把门打开，出去以后再算。

English

The market map marks an old railway signal workshop in the eastern shanty lane. When Aleksei opens the door, the traders' 6.66 MHz code is gone; the shortwave set on the table has just gone silent from 4.17 MHz. Batteries, a hand crank, and a manor plan pressed under the dial show that this fixed transmission point has been in use for a long time. OSK's listening unit is tracking the frequency; every few seconds on air tightens the search around it.

The woman behind the door does not raise a gun. She switches off the transmitter and removes her headphones. She is Yelena, Commissioner Zhukov's daughter, and the one who built the secret broadcast. In brief, low-power bursts on 4.17 MHz, she tells a medical rescue channel outside the city that those whose fevers broke are still alive. OSK is triangulating the frequency. She keeps the transmitter in this fixed workshop and has preserved a route into Pine Manor through the drainage duct.

Yelena wore her fair hair long and a well-tailored winter coat of heavy cloth, the fur trim at the collar and cuffs kept clean. She looked far more presentable than the people outside, yet there was none of the manor's ease on her face.

I've sent the message many times. When someone finally answered, I shut the transmitter off first. My father has the ledgers and Boxcar Three's records locked in the manor. The drainage grate is loose. I'll take you in.

You know they're already tracking this frequency?

I know. I've folded the antenna away. Open the door first. We'll count the cost after we get out.

**【抉择 3-2A：接过频率表】** - **带叶莲娜同行**：阿列克谢接过频率表。叶莲娜加入队伍；她可以命令最近且视线可达的 OSK 停步 8 秒，冷却 25 秒。她给出的排污路线解锁松林庄园。 - **拒绝她的帮助**：阿列克谢只抄下排污路线，不让叶莲娜同行。松林庄园仍可进入，但她不会成为伙伴。

茹科夫专员 诺沃斯克-17 的主管道早就烂透了。主脑算得很清楚：煤炭储备只够撑三十天，药品只够供应六百人，而城里焊死在筒子楼里的活人有整整四万。如果不把三万人打成‘已注销’，如果不把他们送进矿坑，全城的蒸汽三天之内就会彻底耗尽。到时候，所有人——包括你、你妈、我和我的女儿，全都会冻死在床板上。三十箱药分给三百人，你信不信今晚半夜他们就能拿菜刀互相把门砸烂？……我不是恶人，莫罗佐夫。恶人早冻死了，留下来的是算账的。把门焊死，牺牲一部分人，剩下的至少能活到开春。

阿列克谢 （握紧撬棍，声音像从冰缝里挤出来） 可第三节车厢里的人，他们的烧已经退了！他们是活人！

茹科夫专员 （冷漠地看着他） 在电传机里，划掉的名字就是死人。如果他们是活人，那我们过去这一个月算什么？算刽子手？……莫罗佐夫，机器不能犯错。机器一犯错，整座城就会像雪崩一样塌掉。把枪放下，去地下室挑两箱牛排和一箱透析液带走，回你的 602 室去。就当这世上只有你母亲一个人。

叶莲娜 刚才那车透析液推进炉子时，你闻见了吗？外头有人为半勺麦糊告密。你把救命的药烧了，让我在这二十六度的屋子里喝茶。

茹科夫专员 你以为这二十六度是怎么来的？门外那三万人进来，先拆暖气，再来扯你身上这件大衣。我划掉那些名字，就是为了让你还有命站在这里。

叶莲娜 别替我算这笔账。门是我开的，水是我递的，4.17 MHz 的短波也是我架的。你说划掉的名字就是死人。好，写叶莲娜。盖你的红章，叫他们把我也装进第三节。

English

Pine Manor. This was the privileged compound of the railway bureau's cadres.

In brutal contrast to the minus-thirty cold outside, inside the high walls behind a four-metre high-voltage electric fence the radiators ran scalding hot and the air reached twenty-six degrees.

Aleksei slipped through the sewage duct into the No. 2 climate-controlled store. Beneath the glare of incandescent lamps stood neat rows of Swiss-imported Batch-409 dialysis fluid, refined white flour, crated New Zealand butter, and frozen steaks marbled with fat.

Logistics Chief Viktor was directing four workers in white chemical-protection suits as they shovelled a whole cartload of near-expiry peritoneal dialysis fluid and steaks into a roaring industrial incinerator. Black, greasy smoke climbed the towering chimney into the night sky and turned into tar-black snow drifting down over the whole city.

The whole city informs on its neighbours every day for half a teaspoon of cracked grain - and you're shovelling life-saving medicine into the furnace?!

Viktor recoiled in fright and knocked over the cart. Out of the shadows came Commissioner Zhukov, the man who oversaw the privileged zone of Pine Manor, a teacup in his hand. He wore a well-tailored wool uniform, and there was no cruelty on his face - only a weariness and coldness that went to the bone.

Dispatcher Morozov. Do you take me for an embezzler? Or a madman who kills for pleasure?

Zhukov pulled out a drawer, took out a ration-audit master ledger stamped all over with official seals, and threw it down at Aleksei's feet.

Novorsk-17's main pipeline rotted through long ago. The central brain worked it out precisely: the coal reserves would last thirty days, the medicine could supply six hundred people - and there were forty thousand living people sealed into the Khrushchyovka blocks. Unless thirty thousand of them were written off as 'deregistered', unless they were sent into the mines, the city's steam would be utterly exhausted within three days. When that happened, everyone - you, your mother, me, my daughter - would freeze to death on their bedboards. Divide thirty crates of medicine among three hundred people, and I promise you, by midnight tonight they'd be battering down each other's doors with kitchen knives. ... I'm not a villain, Morozov. The villains froze to death long ago. What's left are the ones who keep the accounts. Weld the doors shut, sacrifice a portion, and the rest may live at least until spring.

But the people in the third carriage - their fevers have broken! They're alive!

On the teletype, a name crossed out is a dead man. If they're alive, then what have we been for the past month? Executioners? ... Morozov, the machine cannot make mistakes. Once the machine makes a mistake, the whole city comes down like an avalanche. Put the gun down, go down to the cellar, pick out two crates of steak and one of dialysis fluid, and take them back to your Room 602. Just pretend your mother is the only person left in the world.

With Yelena present, she presses the pass onto the ledger. The brass badge taps the saucer and tea spills over the rim.

Where do you think those twenty-six degrees came from? They’ll tear out the radiators and then that coat off your back. I crossed out those names to keep you standing here.

Don't balance this for me. I opened the door, I brought the water, and I set up the 4.17 MHz transmitter. You said a crossed-out name was a dead person. Fine. Write Yelena. Stamp it red and have them put me in Boxcar Three.

The nib hangs above the empty line. Ink spreads into the paper. Zhukov’s hand moves an inch toward the bell, then stops.

Aleksei. Now.

阿列克谢 （用手电照向墙上的工程图纸） 内务部下令炸山？

博伊科 （惨笑了一声，声音虚弱嘶哑） 上面说……只要炸塌这条巷道，把爆破面彻底封死，矿上就没事了，外面的人也不用天天查体温了。任务代号叫‘净账’……多好听的名词啊。只要山一塌，把里面的一百七十六个人一起埋在里头，账面上的人数和实存人数就彻底‘闭合’了。可我儿子就在里头的掘进二队……他才十九岁，昨天他的高烧刚退下去，他还隔着铁门管我要烟抽……阿廖沙……我按不下去这颗雷管，可我也拆不掉它。巡逻队还有十五分钟交班，我要是没按，他们进来就会把我和我儿子一起当成感染者用喷火器烧死……

In the dispatcher's chamber at the bend of the main haulage way, a carbide lamp hissed with dim yellow light.

A dozen or more crates of military thermite incendiary charges were stacked in neat rows along both sides of the rails, their firing cable running all the way to a green ten-circuit hand-cranked detonator on the workbench.

Boyko, the technician left on watch, sat slumped beside the detonator. English

On the eve of the breakout, a severe overload short-circuited the ground-floor distribution room in Workers' District 3, setting fire to the scrap lumber and discarded chemical-protection suits stacked in the corridor.

Acrid black smoke poured up through the elevator shaft and stairwells, turning the entire Khrushchyovka block into a sealed furnace.

Across the building's only ground-floor fire exit lay a half-ton I-beam welded in place—seven days earlier, a work crew had sealed it there "to ensure the lockdown held tight."

More than a hundred residents were trapped on the smoke-choked stair landings, the screams of women and children all but tearing the night open. From the emergency intercom on the wall, the District Rationing Committee's teletype kept droning the same bulletin: [Fire-break application received · Current approval routing number: 47 · All residents are to remain indoors and wait].

In the smoke, sixty-three-year-old Grandmother Lyudmila was trampled on the third-flight steps. Her face was black with soot; she shielded her five-year-old granddaughter Dyana against her chest.

Aleksei charged up the stairs with a wet cloth over his mouth and nose and caught the old woman by the arm.

Lyudmila coughed violently, shoved Aleksei aside, and thrust the trembling little girl into his arms. Pushed along with her was the soot-blackened denunciation coupon—of the three black characters "informer" on it, only a ragged half-corner remained.

The old woman turned and flung herself at the broken wooden railing, taking the full force of a burning beam that rolled down from above on her back.

Dyana lay against Aleksei's neck, her little face black with smoke, her small hands clutching his collar; tears had carved two white tracks through the soot. She did not cry out. She only whispered into his ear, very softly:

At the ground-floor door, Old Pavel, a Grade-7 fitter, was half-kneeling, a high-pressure oxy-acetylene torch spitting blue-white flame in his hands, burning furiously through the steel spine of the welded beam against the rolling smoke!

Hsss—!!

Sparks flew; the last weld gave way under a hammer blow. More than a hundred soot-faced residents finally broke the iron door and poured out into the blizzard like a flood, stumbling and rolling into the snow. Ash bounded into the drifts, caught the back of Dyana's padded coat in its teeth, and dragged the girl safely out of the wind.

拉娜 我听见你在门外喊我，还以为是梦。门边的几个能走。哥，里头还有人在喘气。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 车厢外的雪崖高坡上，两道冰冷刺眼的探照灯骤然打下！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢把莫辛纳甘举到肩上，准星停在崖顶的督察身上。他没有开枪。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 身穿重型黑色防化服的清杀队督察专员站在履带装甲车顶端，手持扩音器，声音如同齿轮般不带任何人类感情：

督察专员 莫罗佐夫，认得 004 上的签名吗？放下枪，把底单交出来。我可以把你那笔调拨改成戴罪立功，给你和拉娜两张过卡证，让你们活着离开这条线。你不肯？那就和车里的人一起算：你会被登记成盗枪、劫车的叛乱者；这 176 个已经销账的人，天亮前一个也不会剩下。各肃清小队就位！核对第三节车厢档案号 0401 至 0576！准备投掷铝热剂燃烧弹，就地清除！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 崖顶的高架机枪发出拉栓的脆响。

English

A thirty-meter-deep ravine of snow and fallen timber at Eagle's Crag.

The armoured train Buran lay at the bottom like a steel python slit open from belly to tail. Black smoke billowed from its shattered windows; the heavy cast-iron bogies were broken into pieces, and the high-pressure air-brake lines hissed in a final, dying shriek.

The moment the flashlight beam swept inside, it caught the top of the doorframe—a brass nameplate, polished bright by wind and snow, stabbed at his eyes.

The warm afternoon of Act Zero flashed back in fragments. "Sign it, and this week's quota is over target… your mother's crate of medicine goes up to your building first thing tomorrow…"

There had been no other executioner. From the moment he had bowed his head and signed in the dispatch office ten days ago for his mother's sake, the man who had sent his sister aboard that death train with his own hands and nailed the lock onto that door had been Aleksei himself.

At last the flashlight lit the inside of the car.

No mangled monsters. No raving dead.

More than a hundred people sat packed inside. Their padded coats were in rags, their faces covered in frostbite sores—but the Grey Scale on the backs of their hands had all sloughed off, leaving ordinary skin. They huddled together in twos and threes for warmth; a mother wiped her child's tears, and a young worker caught the meltwater dripping from the roof in an aluminium mess tin.

Every one of them was a living person who had come through the high fever.

In the far corner, against the ventilation slit, a gaunt young girl struggled to raise her head. She was wrapped in a man's heavy padded vest stained with machine oil, and the last small patch of dry, peeling scale still clung to her left wrist.

…Alyosha?… Brother!!

Tears broke out of her hollowed eyes in an instant. Reckless, she crawled toward the door, reaching out through the snow with hands covered in chilblains and scratches:

On the high snowbank outside the car, two icy searchlights slammed down!

An OSK commissioner in a heavy black chemical-protection suit stood atop a tracked armoured carrier, megaphone in hand, his voice as flat and joyless as turning gears:

On the cliff above, the heavy machine gun's bolt clicked back.

**【终局闸 D（Endgame Gate D）· 灵魂的最终决断】** - **👉 D1【砸锁抢人，杀上 302 高地广播塔】** 阿列克谢一把将拉娜拉进怀里，背在背上。切尔诺夫中士扯下内务部白袖标；阿列克谢抬起莫辛纳甘，一枪击碎主探照灯。崖顶陷入黑暗，车厢里的人趁机冲出。突围队伍沿检修线直扑 302 广播高地。（进入第六幕，通往隐藏真结局或结局 B。） - **👉 D2【顺从挂锁，闭合账面】** 在极度的崩溃与恐惧下，阿列克谢放下莫辛纳甘，松开妹妹的手。他听从督察专员的命令退后三步，亲手替车厢拉上粗铁栓。铝热剂燃烧弹在车厢内炸开，火光将雪谷照得通红。（直接进入**结局 A · 击毙仁慈**。） - **👉 D3【独自逃离，隐入极夜】** 面对重机枪的火网，阿列克谢丧失了所有勇气。他把莫辛纳甘丢进雪里，松开拉娜，顺着排污涵洞独自爬回城市地窖。（直接进入**结局 C · 荒诞麻木**。）

帕维尔老爹的血肉闸刀6-1 / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 302 高地广播发射塔。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 狂暴的暴风雪拍打着机房巨大的弧形防爆玻璃。外围防线上，装甲运兵车的柴油发动机在低沉咆哮，清杀队的加重穿甲弹将配电室的水泥砖打得碎屑横飞。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 轰！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 一发穿甲弹打穿了外墙，瞬间击碎了主配电盘的绝缘高压瓷瓶！粗大的铜闸刀在高压短路中断开，跳出一团爆燃的蓝白火球！机房中央那两排两米高的巨型电子管发射机红灯骤灭，全城广播网络陷入死寂！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 突击队的防寒靴声已经逼近楼梯拐角。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 七级老钳工帕维尔老爹抹了一把嘴角的血沫子，一把抄起他用了四十年的重型绝缘包胶管钳，猛地卡进了铜闸刀断开的触点之间！

帕维尔老爹 （扭过头，用充血的眼睛盯着阿列克谢和浑身发抖的拉娜） 阿廖沙！去台子前面！把麦克风拧开！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 老人吸了一口气，将全身一百六十斤的体重狠狠压在管钳手柄上，用自己的胸膛和肩膀压住铜闸，硬生生将断开的万伏电闸压回了通电触槽！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 呲啦——轰！！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 万伏高压电弧顺着管钳炸开，狂暴的电流在老人的双臂和胸口爆出焦糊的青烟。帕维尔的双脚死死蹬在水泥机座的边缘，脖颈上的青筋暴起如铁丝，浑身剧烈颤抖，像一尊铸死在配电盘前的乌拉尔钢铁雕塑！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 电子管发射机在一秒钟内重新亮起刺眼的血红光芒！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 全城二十四个工人居住区、三千个锈迹斑斑的铁皮高音喇叭，在午夜的风雪中被同时接通！

English

The Hill 302 radio transmission tower.

A raging blizzard hammered at the great curved blast-proof glass of the transmitter room. Along the outer defence line, armoured personnel carriers growled their diesel low; OSK heavy armour-piercing rounds chewed the distribution room's brickwork into flying chips.

Boom!

An armour-piercing round punched through the outer wall and shattered the high-voltage porcelain insulators of the main switchboard! The heavy copper knife switch tore open on short circuit, bursting into a ball of blue-white flame. The red lamps of the two rows of towering two-meter vacuum-tube transmitters in the middle of the room died at once, and the citywide broadcast network fell silent.

The assault team's winter boots were already closing on the stairwell corner.

Old Pavel, the Grade-7 fitter, wiped the blood foam from the corner of his mouth, seized the heavy insulated pipe wrench he had used for forty years, and jammed it between the open contacts of the copper switch.

Alyosha! Get to the console! Switch the microphone on!

The old man drew a breath and threw all his weight—160 jin—onto the handle, pressing his chest and shoulders against the copper blade, forcing the ten-thousand-volt switch back into its live slot.

Kzzz—boom!!

The ten-thousand-volt arc exploded along the wrench; furious current burst from the old man's arms and chest in scorched blue smoke. His feet braced hard against the edge of the concrete plinth, the veins on his neck standing out like wire, his whole body shaking—an Ural statue of steel cast in place before the switchboard.

Within a second the vacuum-tube transmitters blazed back to eye-searing crimson!

Across the city, twenty-four workers' districts and three thousand rusted tin loudspeakers were live at once in the midnight blizzard!

碎裂的自白与实名点名6-2 / 文学底稿（可与战役对白对照）

旁白 / 阿列克谢 > 只播档案号　拉娜把手放在名单上。阿列克谢播出档案号和罪证。窗边的听众听见自己等了一整夜的数字，铅笔仍悬在纸上。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 > 播出姓名　发射机先吐出一阵电流声，吞掉了拉娜姓氏的头一个音。她扶住麦克风，等那阵噪声过去，从头念了一遍。

拉娜 拉娜·莫罗佐娃。档案号 0420。有人在等我的话……我在这儿。

安娜 我是安娜。档案号 0419。我见过他们退烧。有人被带走的时候还能走，还有人得扶着。表上找不到的事，我记得。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 突击队的撞门木撞裂了机房木门。阿列克谢跃出掩体，莫辛纳甘的枪托砸偏了冲锋枪的枪口，左肩中弹。他单膝跪在发射台前，一把扯过麦克风。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 没有冗长的讲稿，没有四字成语的排比。他嘴里呛着血沫，每呼吸一口，肺部都发出破风箱般的嘶鸣：

阿列克谢 （喘着粗气，声音嘶哑而坚决） ……我是货运站调度员阿列克谢。我怀里揣着一块铜铭牌……第 004 号车皮调拨单……拉娜失踪前十天，为了给我妈换一箱透析液，我亲手签的字……

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢从怀里掏出那块带血的黄铜铭牌，狠狠贴在麦克风的铁丝网上。金属碰撞发出刺耳的啸叫：

阿列克谢 就是这块单子，把我妹妹锁进了车厢……他们从来不要你们服从，他们要你们跟他们一样，手也沾上血，变成共犯！可我现在把底账念给你们听——

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢翻开那本被血染透的《工作日志》背面，用沾满血迹的手指指着上面每一个亲手记下的名字：

阿列克谢 南库 3 号柱，谢尔盖还活着；他的女儿玛丽亚昨夜冻死了。账本把他们都记成零，可他们都有名字！ 白桦林 402 室，莉莉娅！活人！ 东街食堂，瓦西里！死前喊的是渴！不是怪物，是活人！ 601 室，柳德米拉！她为了外孙女告密，换来的券连门都出不去！

旁白 / 阿列克谢 阿列克谢把断面岩样举到电台信号灯下。样本上沾着灰黑粉末，没人能说清它是什么。

阿列克谢 还有——你们说矿工发病是外面带进来的，可工程台账上写着：事故那天，3 号矿坑用了温压炸药，炸穿了冻土。录音里的人紧接着咳起来，喊那不是煤粉。是谁下的命令，谁就该出来解释。内务部却把一百七十六个人写成销账数字，还要用铝热剂把矿坑封死。他们想把人和记录一块埋了。灰粉是什么，我不知道；我只知道他们明明有记录，却要把人和记录一起烧掉。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 高音喇叭的声浪回荡在整座西伯利亚冰城的夜空下。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 赫鲁晓夫楼的走廊里，一个个原本反锁的防盗门被从内侧拉开。人们走出家门，手里握着修铁轨的撬棍、大锤、砍柴的斧头和铁皮扳手。

旁白 / 阿列克谢 年轻的清杀哨兵科利亚端着步枪站在高墙上，看着潮水般涌上铁轨的工人，手里的枪慢慢垂了下去。

English

No long speech, no set phrases in parallel rows. Blood foamed in his mouth; every breath came out of his lungs with the shriek of a broken bellows:

…I'm Aleksei, a dispatcher at the freight station. I've got a brass nameplate here in my coat… Dispatch Slip No. 004… Ten days ago I signed it myself, to buy Mama Katya a crate of dialysis fluid…

Aleksei pulled the bloodstained brass plate from his coat and slammed it against the microphone's wire mesh. Metal shrieked against metal:

This slip is what locked my sister in that boxcar… They never wanted your obedience. They wanted you to be like them—blood on your hands too, accomplices! But now I'm reading you the real ledger—

Aleksei turned to the blood-soaked back pages of his work logbook and pointed with a bloodied finger at every name he had written there with his own hand:

Pillar No. 3, South Depot — Sergei and Maria! Alive! Room 402, Birch Grove — Liliya! Alive! East Street Canteen — Vasily! What he cried out as he died was "Thirsty!" Not a monster. A living man! Room 601 — Lyudmila! She informed on a neighbour for her granddaughter, and the coupon she got in return wouldn't even get her out the door!

The wave of sound from the loudspeakers rolled back and forth beneath the night sky of the whole Siberian ice city.

In the corridors of the Khrushchyovka blocks, door after locked door was pulled open from the inside. People came out of their apartments carrying railway crowbars, sledgehammers, firewood axes, and tin wrenches.

On the high wall, a young OSK sentry, Kolya, held his rifle on the workers pouring onto the tracks like a tide—and slowly let the barrel drop.

台词审计

范围决定理由或落点
0-1 签发、1-1 公寓及七页开场保留现版顺序签发、征召蓝印是两件事。蓝印的发放和用途在冷库登场时交代，开场画面继续对应调度单和 602。
1-2 冷库保留短对白和既有分支用编号和递毯子的动作区分中士与父女。长篇自辩会遮住选择，灰烬仍按冷库结果入队。
1-3 退烧母亲采纳心声和枪前辩解先刻画她在求开门，再给「我退烧了」。枪声随倒地页出现，末页只留物件，镜头保持连续。
1-4 帕沙保留求药和眼前风险援助阶段只说这个晚上，枪口动向和防化服留给之后的余波。
1-4A 扎哈尔保留已有的配给站履历和饥饿动机招募时省去背叛预告和玩家条件。若背叛实际发生，其结果由后续事件交代。
2-1 东窗、601保留短句，文学段保留供审阅空勺、封签和粮券已经交代矛盾。整段可作文学对照，现有战役入口没有独立 601 幻灯。
2-2、2-3 食堂保留父子身份、临终渴水和回访卡刀 QTE 后才交代临终。候选稿假定玩家手拿撬棍、瓦西里滑倒，无法保证每次实况都成立。
2-4 疗养院保留水壶、退烧和 0420 线索安娜的证词成立，长段医学解释、独立缝衣分支均保留为文学候选。
3-1 卡佳分水岭保留现有存活、等待、空床变体附件把车票、遗言和自行停药写成已发生事件。当前存活检查与过场没有实现这些动作。
3-2 黑市、3-2A 叶莲娜保留时刻、排污检修路线和发报动机伊万只交代分工。文学里的破坏细节和过长的罪感独白不进入游戏。
3-3 庄园采纳通行证、开门和递水叶莲娜同行才出现父女对峙。三条任务路径仍由玩家选。
4-1、4-2 矿坑以便携磁带记录器承载三条现场警告，并保留官方庆功标语紧急信息改为录音后，矿工的求救声与墙上的宣传口径形成对照；灰黑粉尘成分和病因保持未知，证物只证明爆破记录与矿井事故。
5-1 火场保留审批广播、托孤和粮券不用“活该”等道德判词替柳德米拉定性，帕维尔和灰烬按实际同行变体出现。
5-2 鹰嘴崖采纳 004 签字回扣及拉娜看见的人0420 是档案号，004 是车皮编号。开门见到活人后再决定救援范围。
5-2 D2 封门不采纳属于早期文学分支，会改写当前开门后的救援规则、拉娜结局及 A 线条件。
6-1、6-2 广播保留广播证据与姓名两种做法帕维尔扶开关按同伴状态出现。省去无条件牺牲和在播报前断言所有人归宿。
602 日常、战役节点和伙伴反应保留日常，采纳调车场伙伴短句伊万盯手、古丽娅先看伤员、安娜先叫拉娜、帕维尔开门、叶莲娜对图、灰烬观察入口、扎哈尔用旧签单反问。对白均发生在到达鹰嘴崖前，省去先行宣布救援和广播的说法。
暖气井、清杀队喊话保留现有文案喂药、绕开和搜掠对应自己的遭遇。临战喊话要能被即时听懂，长独白不适合战斗节奏。
七组随机遭遇保留原来可执行的选项和回执马卡尔和镜中人可供另轮措辞打磨。附件中的药品交换、道具收取、伤势等细节不能当作已实现结果。镜像也无须直接借入文学角色姓名。
A 留在 602改写合上日志、母亲问来信、收音机重复公报。现行 A 不要求封车，也不以火烧车厢作主图。
B 荒诞解放改写三种记录只带拉娜时问车门。接应能走的人时用她给哥哥让半步。旧档撤离范围不明，只写兄妹下楼。省去拉娜死亡或认不出哥哥的说法。
C 麻木改写空床、滴水和收起未写完的日志。此路未必到过鹰嘴崖，省去钉 004 铭牌与瓦西里耳鸣。
α 你好，拉娜改写余波602 的灯、热水、底单和旧钢笔承接家庭重聚与签发责任。展出底单沿用实际路线证物。
β 荒原之火改写失母余波，保留存活变体失母时用围巾与档案收束。卡佳仍在时保留兄妹守火并寻找返程的场景，不写雪坟。
帕沙余波采纳扣子和风雪仅两项既有标志同时满足时追加。把这段留在结局幻灯，避免摘要先把故事讲完。
